        Idea:
# Multi-gateway Discord refactor — brief

**Profile:** `thoughtful//medium +feedback` (tier 3, standard robustness, medium depth, with feedback scaffolding)
**Branch:** `discord-multi-gateway` (cut from `rosi-pregnancy-bot`)
**Sprint shape:** 1–2 focused days; cross-cutting plumbing in known files

---

## 1. Goal

Support running **multiple Discord bot identities in a single FastAPI process**, one per logical bot, so that:

- **Mediator** continues to listen as its existing Discord identity (current `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`)
- **Tante Rosi** listens as a separate Discord identity (Discord application ID `1401572203954311229`)
- Future logical bots can register their own Discord identity by adding (a) a `channels` row, (b) an env var with their token, and (c) a process restart — no further code changes

The work is **wiring, not contract definition**. The routing schema (`channels(transport, address, bot_id)`, `bot_bindings`, `routing.resolve_bot`) is already multi-bot-aware. The runtime is not.

## 2. Non-goals

- Refactoring the WhatsApp transport (still single-provider; out of scope)
- Changing the `bots` / `channels` / `bot_bindings` schema (already correct)
- Changing how scopes resolve (`_resolve_scope` already keys on the bot's own address)
- Removing the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var (kept as fallback during cutover — see §6)
- Onboarding Tante Rosi end-to-end (this brief covers infra only; the prod `bots` + `channels` + `bot_bindings` row inserts happen in a follow-up)
- Inbound WhatsApp routing (mediator's primary prod path may still be WhatsApp; that path is unchanged)

## 3. Current state (what's there today)

### 3.1 One token, one gateway

`app/main.py:185-190` constructs exactly one `DiscordGatewayBot` when `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord`:

```python
if settings.messaging_provider.strip().lower() == "discord":
    await discord.catch_up_recent_messages(pool, app.state.coalescer)
    discord_bot = discord.DiscordGatewayBot(pool, app.state.coalescer)
    app.state.discord_bot = discord_bot
    discord_task = asyncio.create_task(discord_bot.run_forever())
```

That gateway authenticates with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` via the module-level `_token()` helper at `app/services/discord.py:50`. All REST helpers (`send_text`, `add_reaction`, `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `send_typing`, `send_template`, `get_dm_channel_id`, `_rest_client`) read the same token from settings.

### 3.2 Module-level singletons

`app/services/discord.py` keeps `_client: httpx.AsyncClient | None` as a module global, initialised once in `init_client()`. A single client, a single token, a single auth header.

### 3.3 Scope resolution already keys on bot's own address

In `_handle_message` and `_handle_reaction_add` (`app/services/discord.py:478-481`):

```python
bot_user_id = discord_bot_user_id()
scope = await _resolve_scope(self.pool, 'discord', bot_user_id) if bot_user_id else None
```

`routing.resolve_bot('discord', bot_user_id)` looks up the matching `channels` row → returns `bot_id`. This already supports multiple bots; the missing piece is having multiple gateways open at once so DMs to different identities reach the process at all.

### 3.4 Pacer is a singleton too

`DiscordPacer` is constructed once in `app/main.py:120` and stored at `app.state.discord_pacer`. It holds in-memory typing state keyed by `user_id`. Its constructor takes `send_typing=discord.send_typing` — a module-level callable bound to the one token.

### 3.5 Call sites of `discord.*` helpers

Outbound helpers are called from:

- `app/services/messaging.py:260, 417` — `discord.send_text` (the main outbound path)
- `app/services/agentic.py:422` — `discord.add_reaction`
- `app/services/tools/write_tools.py:1912, 1967, 2011` — `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `add_reaction`
- `app/main.py:69, 87, 115, 123` — pacer/coalescer glue calling `get_dm_channel_id`, `add_reaction`

None of these call sites currently knows or threads `bot_id`. They route by `user.phone` (which is the Discord user ID of the recipient).

## 4. Target state

### 4.1 Env var convention

```
DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID_UPPER>   # one per registered Discord bot
DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN                   # legacy fallback — see §6
```

Examples:
- `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`
- `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI`

The `<BOT_ID>` segment is the same `bots.id` value (`mediator`, `tante_rosi`) uppercased and with non-alphanumeric replaced by `_`. The convention is mechanical so adding a new bot needs no code change.

### 4.2 Per-bot Discord client

Introduce a `DiscordClient` class that owns:
- One `bot_id`
- One token (`SecretStr`)
- One `httpx.AsyncClient` (its own connection pool)
- One bot user ID (decoded from token)
- All the REST methods currently in `app/services/discord.py` as **instance methods**

`DiscordGatewayBot` becomes a thin wrapper around a `DiscordClient` plus the websocket gateway loop. One `DiscordGatewayBot` per registered Discord bot.

### 4.3 Module-level helpers — facade pattern

Existing module-level helpers (`discord.send_text`, `discord.add_reaction`, …) stay, but become **facades that require a `bot_id`** (or accept `bot_id=None` and look it up from context — see §4.5). The facade routes to the correct `DiscordClient` instance from a registry.

Pseudocode:
```python
# app/services/discord.py
_clients: dict[str, DiscordClient] = {}

def get_client(bot_id: str) -> DiscordClient:
    return _clients[bot_id]

async def send_text(to: str, body: str, *, bot_id: str, send_typing_indicator: bool = True) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return await get_client(bot_id).send_text(to, body, send_typing_indicator=send_typing_indicator)
```

### 4.4 Per-bot client registry, populated at startup

In `lifespan`:

1. Query `channels WHERE transport='discord'` → list of `(bot_id, address)` pairs
2. For each: read env var `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID>` (or fall back to `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` if exactly one bot row matches — see §6)
3. Instantiate `DiscordClient(bot_id, token)` and register in `_clients[bot_id]`
4. Instantiate `DiscordGatewayBot(bot_id, client, pool, coalescer)` and start the gateway task
5. Append the task to `app.state.background_tasks`

If a `channels` row exists with no matching env var, **log a warning and skip that bot** (do not fail startup — let mediator keep running even if Rosi's token is missing).

### 4.5 Threading `bot_id` through outbound call sites

Two paths to choose from in the plan:

**Option A (recommended): thread `bot_id` through.** Every call site to `discord.send_text`/`add_reaction`/etc gains a `bot_id` parameter. The caller derives `bot_id` from context:
- `messaging.py` outbound path → `bot_id` comes from the `messages.bot_id` of the row being replied to (or from the agentic turn's resolved scope)
- `agentic.py` add_reaction → from the resolved scope
- `write_tools.py` edit/delete/react → from the tool-call's resolved scope (the bot doing the call)
- `main.py` pacer glue → from the user's resolved scope (typing-state-per-bot)

**Option B (worse): infer `bot_id` from `user.phone` via a routing lookup.** Adds DB calls per send; not great.

The plan should pick A and identify exactly where each call site sources `bot_id` from. If any call site has ambiguous context (no obvious source for `bot_id`), surface it in the plan and propose a resolution.

### 4.6 Pacer state

`DiscordPacer` keeps typing state keyed by `user_id`. With two bots, the same user could be typing to mediator AND to Rosi simultaneously, and those should not collide.

**Decision needed in plan:** either
- **Per-bot pacer instances** (`app.state.discord_pacers: dict[str, DiscordPacer]`) — simpler reasoning, more memory
- **Single pacer, key state by `(bot_id, user_id)`** — denser but requires touching every `mark_user_typing` / `typing_state` / `clear_user_typing` signature

Recommend per-bot pacer instances; the memory cost is trivial.

### 4.7 `discord_id.py`

`discord_bot_user_id()` becomes `discord_bot_user_id(bot_id: str)` and reads the per-bot token. The fallback to decode from `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` becomes per-bot env-var lookup with the same decoder.

## 5. Touch points (the full list)

The 12 files/areas the refactor touches:

| # | File | What changes |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `app/services/discord.py` | Introduce `DiscordClient` class. Convert module-level REST helpers to facades. `DiscordGatewayBot` becomes per-bot. Module-level `_client` global removed. |
| 2 | `app/services/discord_id.py` | `discord_bot_user_id()` takes `bot_id`. New env-var convention. |
| 3 | `app/config.py` | Per-bot token discovery. Settings exposes `discord_bot_tokens: dict[str, SecretStr]` populated from env (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID>` pattern). Keep `discord_bot_token` for back-compat fallback. |
| 4 | `app/main.py` | Lifespan iterates `channels WHERE transport='discord'`, creates per-bot clients and gateways. Pacer becomes a dict keyed by `bot_id`. Pacer-glue closures (`on_live_typing`, `before_paced_send`) thread `bot_id`. |
| 5 | `app/services/messaging.py` | `send_outbound` and `send_outbound_part` thread `bot_id` to `discord.send_text`. Source: the row's `bot_id` or the agentic turn's scope. |
| 6 | `app/services/agentic.py` | `discord.add_reaction` call gains `bot_id`. Source: agentic turn's resolved scope. |
| 7 | `app/services/tools/write_tools.py` | Three call sites (edit/delete/react) gain `bot_id`. Source: each tool's resolved scope from `messages.bot_id`. |
| 8 | `app/services/pacer.py` | If we go per-bot pacer instances (recommended): no signature change. If single-pacer-with-(bot_id, user_id) keys: every method that touches typing state gains `bot_id`. |
| 9 | `scripts/seed_channels.py` | Currently seeds one Discord channel from one token. New behaviour: iterate over a configurable set of (bot_id, token) pairs from env, seed a `channels` row per pair. **Idempotent** — re-running with the same env should be a no-op. |
| 10 | `app/main.py` (startup diagnostics) | `_log_startup_diagnostics` now logs *which* per-bot tokens are configured. Don't log token values. |
| 11 | `app/services/discord.py` (`catch_up_recent_messages` and `seed_partner_users`) | Both currently use single-token REST. `seed_partner_users` is mediator-specific (seeds two partners from `DISCORD_PARTNER_USER_ID_A/B`) — keep that behaviour bound to mediator's client only. `catch_up_recent_messages` becomes per-bot. |
| 12 | Tests | Anything that mocks `discord._token()` or constructs `DiscordGatewayBot()` with the old signature needs to update. Estimated: 6–15 test files. |

## 6. Cutover strategy

The Veas Railway service is **currently live** running mediator on `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN=<mediator's token>` (assumed; verify in prep phase). The deploy must not regress mediator.

### 6.1 Back-compat fallback

During the transition, support **both** env var schemes:

1. New: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`, `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI` (etc.)
2. Legacy: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` (singular) — treated as the token for the **single** `bot_id` in the `channels` table that matches, OR ignored if multiple Discord channels exist.

Logic at startup:
- If any per-bot env var is set, use per-bot lookup.
- If only `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` is set and exactly one `channels` row has `transport='discord'`, treat it as that bot's token (logs a deprecation warning).
- If neither matches the registered channels, log a warning and skip those bots.

This means **prod cutover is a config change, not a code-and-config simultaneous change**:

1. Deploy code with multi-gateway support. Old `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` still works for mediator. No regression.
2. Set `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR=<same value as DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN>` in Railway. Mediator now works under both schemes.
3. Insert `channels` row for `tante_rosi` (with Rosi's Discord bot user ID as address).
4. Set `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI=<Rosi's token>` in Railway.
5. Insert `bots` row + `bot_bindings` row for `tante_rosi`.
6. Restart. Both gateways come up.
7. After verification: delete the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var. Confirm mediator still works (it should, via `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`).

The fallback can be retired in a follow-up PR once everyone's confident.

### 6.2 What `channels` row currently exists?

The prep phase **must verify**: query `mediator.channels WHERE transport='discord'` against prod and report what's there. If a mediator `channels` row already exists with the right `address` (mediator's Discord bot user ID), great. If not, we need to insert one as part of the cutover (the legacy code apparently worked without one because of the `_resolve_scope` mediator default).

### 6.3 Test environment

Local dev / CI typically runs with `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` and may have no Discord token at all. The refactor must not break:
- Startup with no Discord tokens (the gateway-loop section just doesn't run)
- Startup with `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` (Discord init is skipped entirely)
- Tests that mock `discord._token()` or `discord.send_text` (these need updating, but the failure modes should be loud, not silent)

## 7. Acceptance criteria

A reviewer should be able to confirm all of these from the diff + a manual test:

1. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` + only legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` set + one `channels` row → works exactly as before.** Mediator gateway opens, receives messages, replies. No deprecation noise that breaks anything.
2. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` + `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR` + `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI` + two `channels` rows → both gateways open.** Each handles only its own DMs. Replies route to the right bot's token (verify with a DM to each).
3. **Pacer state is per-bot.** Typing to mediator does not affect Rosi's pacer state.
4. **Adding a third bot is purely config:** insert `bots` row, insert `channels` row, set env var, restart. No code change required.
5. **All existing tests pass** with adjustments only to the discord-test surface (no broad test-suite shuffling).
6. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` is unchanged.** No Discord init runs; no errors on missing Discord env vars.
7. **`seed_channels.py` is idempotent under the new scheme** — running it twice with the same env produces no diff in the table.
8. **Startup diagnostics list which bot ids are configured for Discord and which are missing tokens.** No token values in logs.
9. **No regression in pytest collection / type checking.**

## 8. Risks and watchouts

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| **Threading `bot_id` through `messaging.py` is touchier than it looks** because outbound messages can be replies to messages that have NULL `bot_id` (legacy mediator rows). | Default to `'mediator'` when the source row's `bot_id` is NULL. Make this explicit in the plan. |
| **`agentic.py` `add_reaction` call site lacks an obvious `bot_id` source** if the reaction is fire-and-forget. | The agentic turn always has a resolved scope; thread it through. |
| **Pacer signature change ripples** if we go single-pacer-with-keys instead of per-bot-pacer. | Recommend per-bot pacer; signature unchanged. |
| **`init_client` / `close_client` module-level lifecycle** is currently keyed off the one global `_client`. With per-bot clients, the dict is the new state. | The lifespan now closes all clients in the dict. Don't forget the `close_client` call in the `finally:` of `lifespan`. |
| **Cutover env-var ordering** — if `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR` is set but the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` still exists, what wins? | Per-bot env vars always win. Legacy only kicks in when no per-bot match exists for a channel row. Document explicitly. |
| **Tests that mock the module-level `_token()` or `_client`** will break silently if not updated. | Search for these patterns in prep phase; produce a list of test files needing updates. |
| **The decode-from-token helper in `discord_id.py`** is called by `seed_channels.py` and by inbound handlers. Both need per-bot variants. | Pass `bot_id` explicitly to the decoder; the env var pattern handles the rest. |

## 9. Deliverables

A single branch, `discord-multi-gateway`, cut from `rosi-pregnancy-bot`. One PR. Passing test suite. The PR description should include:

- Summary of the refactor
- The new env-var convention
- Cutover steps for prod (copy-paste of §6.1)
- A note on what's NOT in the PR (the `bots` / `channels` / `bot_bindings` row inserts for Tante Rosi are a follow-up — those need the Discord bot ID and the principal user ID, which are operator data)

## 10. Out of scope (don't do these in this PR)

- Inserting prod `bots` row for `tante_rosi`
- Inserting prod `channels` row for `tante_rosi`
- Inserting prod `bot_bindings` row for `tante_rosi`
- Onboarding the principal user to Rosi
- Changing the WhatsApp transport
- Retiring the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var (follow-up after cutover)
- Refactoring the inbound path beyond what's needed to thread `bot_id` through scope resolution (which is already in place)

## 11. References

- `app/services/discord.py:50` — `_token()` singleton
- `app/services/discord.py:329-487` — `DiscordGatewayBot` class
- `app/services/discord.py:478-481` — scope resolution that already keys on bot's own address
- `app/services/routing.py:20` — `resolve_bot(transport, address)`
- `app/services/inbound.py:169-209` — `_resolve_scope`
- `app/main.py:185-190` — single-gateway construction
- `app/main.py:118-145` — `_build_coalescer` and `DiscordPacer` construction
- `migrations/0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql` — `bots`, `channels`, `bot_bindings` schema
- `scripts/seed_channels.py` — currently single-token channel seeder

## 12. What the planner should produce

A plan that:

1. Explicitly proposes the `DiscordClient` class shape (constructor, instance methods, where it lives)
2. Lists the exact signature change for each of the 12 touch points
3. Resolves the per-bot-pacer vs single-pacer question with a reason
4. Specifies how each outbound call site sources `bot_id` (the §4.5 question)
5. Sequences the cutover-back-compat logic correctly (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` fallback under what conditions)
6. Identifies the test files that need updating (prep phase output)
7. Confirms the `seed_channels.py` idempotency strategy under the new scheme
8. Has a clear "this is what 'done' looks like" against §7

The plan is allowed to overrule any specific recommendation in this brief if it has a reason — but it must surface the disagreement explicitly, not silently.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Multi-gateway Discord Refactor (rev 3)

## Overview
Today the FastAPI process opens exactly one Discord gateway authenticated with a single module-level token (`app/services/discord.py:50`). Goal: support N Discord identities in one process via per-bot `DiscordClient` instances and per-bot gateway loops, with `bot_id` threaded through every outbound call site.

Rev 3 closes the remaining 5 open flags from iter 2 (weighted score 25.0 → 12.5):

- **Non-agentic `send_outbound` callers** (`transcription.py:72,113`; `vision.py:193,213`; `scheduled_job_handlers.py:89,114`) explicitly pass `bot_id='mediator'`. These are all mediator-only flows today; making the constant explicit avoids regressing the legacy-token deploy while keeping the agentic path's `ctx.bot_id` discipline. Resolves `FLAG-010`, `correctness` (iter-2), `scope` (iter-2).
- **Lifespan channels query wrapped in try/except** for `UndefinedTableError` (stale dev DB without migration 0020) — falls back to the legacy single-token path. Resolves `all_locations` (iter-2).
- **`ctx.partner.phone` None-guard added in this PR** at `write_tools.py:1911, 1966, 2008-2010` (guards return early with a clear log line for solo bots). Decision committed; no longer deferred. Resolves `callers` (iter-2).
- **FLAG-004 / correctness-4 evidence**: Step 8 now quotes the matching expression index by line (`migrations/0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql:69-70`: `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS channels_uniq_coalesced ON channels (transport, address, COALESCE(guild_id,''), COALESCE(channel_id,''))`). Verified locally against the file — the ON CONFLICT clause IS backed by an existing index. Flags remain disputed-with-evidence.

Branch: `discord-multi-gateway`. Out of scope: prod data inserts for Tante Rosi, WhatsApp changes, legacy env var retirement.

## Phase 1: Audit + supporting infrastructure

### Step 1: Prep audit — enumerate every touch site (PR description deliverable)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Grep** for the soon-to-change surface and capture output for the PR:
   - `rg -n "discord\.(send_text|send_template|send_typing|add_reaction|edit_text|delete_text|get_dm_channel_id|_token|_client|_get_client|init_client|close_client)\b" app/ scripts/ tests/`
   - `rg -n "discord_bot_user_id\(|DiscordGatewayBot\(" app/ scripts/ tests/`
   - `rg -n "send_outbound\(|send_outbound_part\(" app/ scripts/ tests/`
   - `rg -n "DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN\b|DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID\b|DISCORD_PARTNER_USER_ID_[AB]\b" app/ scripts/ tests/`
2. **Produce** PR deliverables: (a) every outbound call site with the `bot_id` source it will use; (b) every test file mocking `discord._token`/`discord._client`/`discord.send_text` or constructing `DiscordGatewayBot(...)`; (c) a list of every non-agentic `send_outbound` caller confirmed mediator-only.
3. **Verify** migration `0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql:69-70` — the unique index `channels_uniq_coalesced` on `(transport, address, COALESCE(guild_id,''), COALESCE(channel_id,''))` exists and matches `scripts/seed_channels.py:79`'s ON CONFLICT clause. Quote the lines in the PR description (rejects `FLAG-004`, `correctness-4`).
4. **Verify** Tante Rosi `tool_allowlist` excludes `edit_message`/`delete_message`/`add_reaction`. Record current allowlist in PR description.
5. **Query prod** (read-only): `SELECT bot_id, address, guild_id, channel_id FROM channels WHERE transport='discord'`. Decision committed: no defensive auto-seed; cutover playbook handles missing rows manually.

### Step 2: Per-bot token discovery (`app/config.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `Settings.discord_bot_tokens() -> dict[str, SecretStr]` — scans env for `^DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_([A-Z0-9_]+)$`, lowercases group to `bot_id`. Read at call time.
2. **Add** `Settings.discord_bot_user_id_overrides() -> dict[str, str]` — scans `^DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_([A-Z0-9_]+)$`. Unsuffixed `DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID` stays mediator-only.
3. **Keep** `discord_bot_token` and `discord_bot_user_id` fields for legacy fallback.

### Step 3: Per-bot bot-user-id (`app/services/discord_id.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Change** signature: `discord_bot_user_id(bot_id: str) -> str | None`. Resolution: (a) `discord_bot_user_id_overrides().get(bot_id)`; (b) decode from `discord_bot_tokens().get(bot_id)`; (c) if `bot_id == 'mediator'`, fall back to legacy `DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID` then `_decode_discord_user_id(DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN)`. Non-mediator: no legacy fallback.
2. **Update callers** per Step 1 audit: gateway path uses `self.client.bot_user_id`; `seed_channels.py` calls `_decode_discord_user_id(token)` directly with per-bot token.
3. **Keep** `_decode_discord_user_id(token)` signature unchanged.

## Phase 2: DiscordClient + facades

### Step 4: Introduce `DiscordClient` class (`app/services/discord.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Create** `class DiscordClient`: `__init__(self, bot_id, token)` → stores `bot_id`, `_token`, builds `_http: httpx.AsyncClient`, computes `self.bot_user_id = _decode_discord_user_id(token)`. `async def aclose(self)` closes `_http`. `_headers(self)`, `_rest_client(self)` are instance methods.
2. **Move** REST helpers as instance methods: `send_text`, `send_template`, `send_typing`, `add_reaction`, `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `get_dm_channel_id`. **Fix `send_template`'s internal `send_text(...)` call** to `self.send_text(...)` (instance-bound) — resolves the broken cross-helper invocation.
3. **Add** module registry: `_clients: dict[str, DiscordClient] = {}`; `register_client(c)`, `get_client(bot_id)`, `iter_clients()`, `async close_all_clients()`.
4. **Replace** module-level helpers with facades requiring `*, bot_id: str`:
   ```python
   async def send_text(to, body, *, bot_id, send_typing_indicator=True):
       return await get_client(bot_id).send_text(to, body, send_typing_indicator=send_typing_indicator)
   ```
   Same pattern for the other 6 helpers.
5. **Remove** module globals: `_client`, `init_client`, `close_client`, `_token`, `_headers`, `_get_client`, `_rest_client`. **Update** `_send_typing_after_delay(channel_id, *, bot_id)` to call the facade.

### Step 5: Per-bot `DiscordGatewayBot` (`app/services/discord.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Change** constructor: `DiscordGatewayBot(bot_id, client: DiscordClient, pool, coalescer)`.
2. **Replace** internal `discord_bot_user_id()` calls with `self.client.bot_user_id`; module-helper calls with `self.client.<method>(...)`.
3. **Fix the pre-existing NameError** at `app/services/discord.py:465-466`: hoist `bot_id`/`topic_id` resolution above the `if target_id is None:` early-return so the log line at `:465-466` has those names in scope.
4. **Document** `_configured_partner_name`'s mediator-scoped env design in a code comment: returns `None` for non-mediator bots, caller falls back to `user_id` (existing safe behavior).
5. **Convert** `catch_up_recent_messages(pool, coalescer, *, client: DiscordClient)`.
6. **Scope** `seed_partner_users(pool)` to mediator only via lifespan gate.

### Step 6: Per-bot `DiscordPacer` (`app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Keep** `DiscordPacer` signature unchanged.
2. **Construct per-bot** with a `bot_id`-binding closure:
   ```python
   def _make_send_typing(bot_id: str):
       async def _send(channel_id: str) -> None:
           await discord.send_typing(channel_id, bot_id=bot_id)
       return _send
   pacer = DiscordPacer(send_typing=_make_send_typing(bot_id))
   ```
3. **Replace** `app.state.discord_pacer` with `app.state.discord_pacers: dict[str, DiscordPacer]`.
4. **Build coalescer-glue closures (`on_live_typing`, `before_paced_send`) per-bot**, each capturing its own `bot_id`/pacer. Glue calls into `discord.get_dm_channel_id`/`discord.add_reaction` pass `bot_id=` from the closure.

### Step 7: Lifespan registration loop (`app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **At startup** (when `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord`): query
   ```python
   try:
       rows = await pool.fetch("SELECT bot_id, address FROM channels WHERE transport='discord'")
   except asyncpg.UndefinedTableError:
       logger.warning("channels table missing; falling back to legacy single-token mediator path")
       rows = []
   ```
   This addresses `all_locations` (iter-2): stale dev DBs without migration 0020 don't crash startup.
2. **Token resolution per row**: `[REDACTED]().get(bot_id)`. If absent AND `len(rows) == 1` AND `settings.discord_bot_token`, use legacy as fallback with deprecation warning (brief §6.1 strict).
3. **If `rows` is empty AND `settings.discord_bot_token` is set**: synthesize a single in-memory mediator entry as the legacy back-compat path (not a synthesized DB row; just an in-process gateway for the legacy deploy). Otherwise log a warning and skip.
4. **For each (bot_id, token)**: instantiate `DiscordClient`, register, build per-bot pacer + glue + gateway, `await catch_up_recent_messages(pool, coalescer, client=client)`, launch via `asyncio.create_task`. Append to `app.state.background_tasks`. If `bot_id == 'mediator'`, call `seed_partner_users(pool)` once.
5. **Teardown** in `finally:`: `await discord.close_all_clients()`.
6. **Diagnostics**: `_log_startup_diagnostics` lists configured bot_ids and which have tokens. No token values.

### Step 8: `scripts/seed_channels.py` — multi-bot iteration
**Scope:** Small
1. **Confirmed** (Step 1.3): existing ON CONFLICT clause matches `channels_uniq_coalesced` at `migrations/0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql:69-70`. Keep as-is structurally.
2. **Rewrite** `seed_discord(pool)` to iterate `get_settings().discord_bot_tokens()` (plus legacy single-mediator fallback). Per `(bot_id, token)`: read `DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_<BOT_ID>` override or decode from token, then run the same `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT ... DO NOTHING` with the row's `bot_id`. Idempotent.
3. **Log** per-bot result.

## Phase 3: Thread `bot_id` through outbound callers

### Step 9: Messaging outbound (`app/services/messaging.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `bot_id: str` as a required keyword arg to `send_outbound` and `send_outbound_part`. The WhatsApp branch ignores it (single-bot transport); the Discord branch threads it to `discord.send_text(..., bot_id=bot_id)`.
2. **Agentic callers**: source from `ctx.bot_id` (TurnContext). Stamp `messages.bot_id` from `ctx.bot_id` on the outbound insert.
3. **Non-agentic callers — enumerated and committed** (resolves `FLAG-010`, `correctness`, `scope`):
   - `app/services/transcription.py:72` — pass `bot_id='mediator'` (audio transcription reply; mediator-only flow today).
   - `app/services/transcription.py:113` — pass `bot_id='mediator'`.
   - `app/services/vision.py:193` — pass `bot_id='mediator'`.
   - `app/services/vision.py:213` — pass `bot_id='mediator'`.
   - `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py:89` — pass `bot_id='mediator'` (scheduled reminders).
   - `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py:114` — pass `bot_id='mediator'` (weekly summaries).
   Define a module constant `MEDIATOR_BOT_ID = 'mediator'` in each file (or import from `app.bots.registry`) and use it at these call sites with a one-line comment explaining "mediator-only flow; promote to scope-derived bot_id when these features extend to multi-bot."

### Step 10: Agentic + write tools (`app/services/agentic.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`agentic.py:422`** (`add_reaction`): pass `bot_id=ctx.bot_id`.
2. **`write_tools.py:1912, 1967, 2011`**: each tool passes `bot_id=ctx.bot_id` (the acting bot).
3. **Add `ctx.partner.phone` None-guard in this PR** (resolves `callers` iter-2). At `write_tools.py:1911, 1966, 2008-2010`, before dereferencing `ctx.partner.phone`, check `if ctx.partner is None: logger.info("tool %s called by solo bot, skipping", tool_name); return ToolResult(...)` (or analogous early-return matching the tool's return contract). Decision: include in this PR so a future allowlist widening cannot cause a prod crash.

### Step 11: `main.py` pacer-glue covered by Step 6
**Scope:** Small
1. No additional work — glue closures from Step 6 already thread `bot_id`.

## Phase 4: Tests and verification

### Step 12: Test infrastructure + new tests
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend `tests/conftest.py` FakePool** to model `SELECT bot_id, address FROM channels WHERE transport='discord'` with configurable rows (default: one mediator row). Also model the `UndefinedTableError` path via a fixture for the stale-DB scenario.
2. **Update** test files enumerated in Step 1: register stub `DiscordClient` in `_clients`; pass `bot_id='mediator'` to facade calls; update `DiscordGatewayBot(...)` constructions.
3. **Add `tests/test_discord_multi_gateway.py`** covering:
   - Two channels rows + two tokens → two clients, two gateway tasks.
   - Facade dispatches by `bot_id`.
   - Legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`-only + one row → mediator gateway with deprecation warning.
   - Channels row exists but no matching token → warning, skip, others boot.
   - Zero rows + legacy token set → in-memory mediator synthesis works.
   - Zero rows + no legacy token → warning, no Discord init, no crash.
   - `UndefinedTableError` on channels query → warning, falls back to legacy path.
   - Per-bot pacer isolation.
   - `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` → no Discord init.
4. **Add `tests/test_messaging_non_agentic_bot_id.py`** verifying transcription/vision/scheduled_job_handlers call `send_outbound` with `bot_id='mediator'`.
5. **Add `tests/test_write_tools_solo_bot_guard.py`** verifying edit/delete/react tools no-op gracefully when `ctx.partner is None`.

### Step 13: Validation
**Scope:** Small
1. `pytest tests/test_discord_multi_gateway.py tests/test_messaging_non_agentic_bot_id.py tests/test_write_tools_solo_bot_guard.py -x`
2. `pytest tests/ -k "discord or messaging or write_tools" -x`
3. `pytest` (full suite)
4. `ruff check . && mypy app/` per repo conventions.

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 (audit + config + discord_id) — surfaces deliverables, no runtime change.
2. Phase 2 (DiscordClient + lifespan + pacer + seed script) — runtime multi-gateway-capable.
3. Phase 3 (thread `bot_id` through agentic AND non-agentic callers; add solo-bot guard).
4. Phase 4 (tests).

## Validation Order
1. Targeted new tests.
2. Discord/messaging/write_tools subset.
3. Full pytest.
4. Lint/type.
5. Manual staging smoke (info-only).

      "id": "DEBT-059",
      "concern": "all_locations: staging.py:47 calls build_hot_context without the new allow_cross_topic_peek/allow_cross_topic_status_injection flags. since these default to false, staging replay of the mediator bot will not exercise cross-topic paths, diverging from production behavior where mediator has both flags true.",

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",

      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    },

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 4.5's `_row_matches_topic` helper falls back to global behavior when (table, id) is not in self.artifact_topics \u2014 explicitly for backward compatibility. this is sensible but creates a subtle hazard: a test that forgets to call link_topic for a new artifact sees global decay behavior with no warning, which could mask the very scoping bug the new test in step 12 is meant to catch. the decay scope test does call link_topic for both topics, so it's safe; future authors may not. worth recording but not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: new issue: the 0020 migration ddl defines `channels` with `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`. postgresql treats null != null under standard unique constraints, so two whatsapp rows with `guild_id=null, channel_id=null` and the same transport+address would not conflict. however, `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, which requires a matching unique index on those coalesce expressions. without it, the seed script will raise 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification'. the ddl should either use `unique nulls not distinct` (pg15+, and the repo uses pg16 per `test_eval_workflow.py:36`) or a unique index on the coalesce expressions.",

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 17,
    "plan_count": 7,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-002",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-011",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-019",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

      {
        "id": "DEBT-026",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-031",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2b-constraints"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-034",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 4.5's `_row_matches_topic` helper falls back to global behavior when (table, id) is not in self.artifact_topics \u2014 explicitly for backward compatibility. this is sensible but creates a subtle hazard: a test that forgets to call link_topic for a new artifact sees global decay behavior with no warning, which could mask the very scoping bug the new test in step 12 is meant to catch. the decay scope test does call link_topic for both topics, so it's safe; future authors may not. worth recording but not a blocker.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-3-join-cutover"

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: flag-017 (decay handler backward-compat fallback) is downgraded to accepted maintainability debt with an inline warning comment per step 4.5. the warning text explicitly tells future authors to call link_topic. acceptable, but a noisy assertion (e.g. log a warning when topic_id present in sql but row has no link) would catch silent regressions more loudly. not blocking.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5's mid-turn refresh refetches user inside the same db transaction (for update held). if the refresh uses fetch_user_by_id rather than dataclasses.replace, it issues a select through the pool while the same connection holds the lock \u2014 with asyncpg pools this can deadlock unless the same acquired connection is reused. plan offers both options without specifying which connection the re-fetch uses. dataclasses.replace avoids this entirely; if executor picks fetch_user_by_id, lock contention risk is real.",

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-001",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [

    "subsystem": "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them",
    "total_occurrences": 8,
    "plan_count": 6,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-003",
        "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: new issue: the 0020 migration ddl defines `channels` with `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`. postgresql treats null != null under standard unique constraints, so two whatsapp rows with `guild_id=null, channel_id=null` and the same transport+address would not conflict. however, `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, which requires a matching unique index on those coalesce expressions. without it, the seed script will raise 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification'. the ddl should either use `unique nulls not distinct` (pg15+, and the repo uses pg16 per `test_eval_workflow.py:36`) or a unique index on the coalesce expressions.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
